<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, May 14, 2019
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, May 14, 2019, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up
to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes:  April 23, 2019  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 April 2019  
 
c. Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, March; Winslow Precinct #2, March; Show Low
Precinct #5, March; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, March 2019
 
 
d. Personnel Actions  
 
e. Approval of 25 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $11,618.24  
 
f. Amendment No. 1 to Contract #F14-05-09 to extend the Inmate Communications Services
with ICSoultions an additional 4 month period (June 9, 2019 to October 31, 2019)
 
 
g. Amendment No. 4 to Contract #B15-04-027  for Hauling Waste Tires extending the contract
one additional year to June 8, 2020
 
 
h. Execute the Right of Way document from the Arizona State Land Department for the
extension of Woodruff Road West of the intersection with SR-77

<!-- page 2 -->
i. Amendment No. 1 to Contract #F15-01-05 to extend the Banking Agreement with Wells
Fargo for one additional year (June 10, 2019 to June 9, 2020)
 
 
j. Show Low High School Cross Country Team request for $1000.00 in Anti-Racketeering
Revolving Funds to use for community based programs that include providing teaching on
substance abuse prevention
 
 
k. Motion to re-appoint Superior Court Judge Pro Tempore David J. Martin  
 
l. FY2020 Fill the Gap Grant application  
 
m. FY20 TCPF Grant Application  
 
n. Reissuance of Stale-Dated Checks  
 
o. Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver  
 
p. Application for supervised fireworks display permit and permit to shoot an anvil submitted
by the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce 
 
 
q. Appointment of Candace Annette Kelley, Jean Brooke Stackey, Mardi Ann Harris, Arthur
Joe Kelley, JR., Wayne Lee Pearce, Luther H. Lee, Susan J. Etsitty, Martha Pearce and Penny
West, as Democratic Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
 
 
r. Purchase of (2) 2019 15-Passenger Vans from Show Low Ford using federal WIOA grant
funds
 
 
s. Memorandum of Agreement between Navajo County Probation Department and The
Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board to cost-share employees
 
 
t. Inmate Housing Agreement between the White Mountain Apache Tribe and Navajo County  
 
u. Revenue Sharing Application to the Pascua Yaqui Tribe by the Heber-Overgaard Fire District
for the Rapid Extraction Module Support (REMS) Program in the amount of $35,818.74 and
Letter of Support signed by the Chairwoman
 
 
v. Intergovernmental Agreement between Arizona Department of Forestry and Fire
Management and Navajo County Board of Supervisors regarding Environmental Program
Assistance 
 
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Child Fatality Review contract amendment to change the contract number from
ADHS19-214846 to CTR041862

<!-- page 3 -->
2. Amendment 1 to the Immunization VFC Grant Contract No: ADHS18-177684, scope of work
is amended to detail quality improvement activities
 
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Presentation to retiring employee, Jeffrey Nafziger, for his nearly 15 years of service to
Navajo County and the Navajo County Health Department. 
 
Presented By: Eric Scott and Jeff Lee
 
b. Health Benefits Update  
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
5. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration of adoption of Resolution Number 14-19, approving the participation in the
Census 2020 Complete Count Committee and to encourage every person to be counted. 
 
Presented By: Bryan Cook/Luis Cardenas/Arbin Mitchell
 
6. TREASURER:  
 
a. Consideration of the Abatement of Personal Property Taxes in accordance with ARS
42-19118 and ARS 42-18353.
 
Presented By: Debra Kester
 
7. INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY:  
 
a. Cyber Security Update  
Presented By: Ken Dewitt
 
8. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring the week of May 19-25, 2019 as National Public
Works Week in Navajo County.
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
b. Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 16 to Memorandum of Agreement between
the Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation- Navajo Region and Navajo
County for the funding of purchasing aggregate material and construction water to be
delivered to BIA route 157 between the Indian Wells and Greasewood Community. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
c. Consideration of authorizing Easement and Land Purchase Agreement with the James M.
Lando Trust for the School House Lane Roadway Easement.
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
d. Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake authorizing
Navajo County to provide financial assistance for the purchase of materials, labor and
equipment to be used to chip seal various streets in the Town of Snowflake. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood

<!-- page 4 -->
e. VICTORY HEIGHTS ROAD MAINTENANCE DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Amendment No. 2 to the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District, Contract #B17-05-016,
for road maintenance and related improvements, extending the contract for one additional
year to July 10, 2020 with no cost increase
 
 
9. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
10. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: May 10, 2019 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 1:15 pm By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.f.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: IC Solutions Contract Extension
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Ryan Larson, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 1 to Contract #F14-05-09 to extend the Inmate Communications Services with
ICSoultions an additional 4 month period (June 9, 2019 to October 31, 2019)
Background:
On June 10, 2014, the Board of Supervisors approved the Contract with Inmate Calling
Solutions, LLC dba ICSolutions for Inmate Communications Services, Contract
#F14-05-09. ICSoultions was the winning bidder and this Contract is set to expire on June
9, 2019. The four month extension will allow adequate time to research new technology
prior to rebidding this contract.  Staff recommends that the contract be extended to
October 31, 2019  with no price increase.  Both the Sheriff and the Contractor are in
concurrence.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.g.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: Approval of Amendment No. 4 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Michelle Johnston, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 4 to Contract #B15-04-027  for Hauling Waste Tires extending the
contract one additional year to June 8, 2020
Background:
On June 9, 2015, the Board of Supervisors approved the Contract with CRM of America,
LLC (CRM) for the Hauling of Waste Tires, Contract #B15-04-027.  CRM was the only
bid received and this contract has worked very well for the County.  This Contract may be
renewed up to four additional one-year terms. This would be the fourth renewal; the
contractor is in concurrence and will renew with no cost increase to the current contract. 
Staff recommends that the contract be extended one additional year (June 9, 2019 to June
8, 2020).
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019/2020
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in the FY 2019 and 2020 budget.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.h.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: Arizona State Land Dept. Right of Way 16-120107: Woodruff Road Extention
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bryan Cook, Sr. Engineering Project Manager II
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Execute the Right of Way document from the Arizona State Land Department for the extension
of Woodruff Road West of the intersection with SR-77
Background:
Navajo County has partnered with Aztec Land and Cattle Company to obtain Right of Way across
state land and to allow for the possibility of future development along the SR-77  corridor. As part
of the agreement, Navajo County would work with the Arizona State Land Department to obtain a
perpetual public Right of Way across State Land at the intersection of Woodruff Road and SR-77
provided that Aztec Land and Cattle Company fund any expenses associated with the application
with the State.
This agreement signifies a successful partnership with Aztec Land and Cattle Company and the
Arizona State Land Department to obtain public easements that may be used as a model for future 
endeavors for other roads or accesses across State Land.

C.  Project Information
13. DESCRIPTION of Project Plan.
SUPERIOR COURT/CLERK OF THE COURT
The project plan is to provide adequate funding to supplement general funds and other funds the Superior Court/Clerk 
of the Court utilize to provide criminal case processing services.
Case Flow Management.  Our Caseflow Manager provides the Judges with caseflow management reports which 
provide pertinent information about case aging, disposition rates, and time to disposition.  The Caseflow Manager 
provides monthly, quarterly and annual report information to the Judges and Court Administrator.
Field Trainer.  Provides county-wide training to general and limited jurisdiction court staff, as well as probation and 
detention staff.  This position also supports the courts with ensuring the adopted time standard reports and DUI reports 
are produced, and also helps the courts identify any discrepencies within the reports so they can be addressed prior to 
submitting them to the AOC.
Language Access.  To provide persons with limited English proficiency (LEP) services that are in compliance with Title 
VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 (42 U.S.C. 2000d et seq.; 45 C.F.R. § 80.1 et seq.; and 28 C.F.R. § 42.101–42.112), 
general funds and other court funds provide funding of a full-time Spanish Interpreter.  However, this position covers 11 
courts in the county, which can lead to calendar conflicts between the Justice and Superior Court.  In order to ensure 
these conflicts do not cause case delay, additional funding for contracted interpreters will help to ensure cases can 
proceed as scheduled without the need to continue them.  Funding will also be utilized for languages other than Spanish 
(i.e. Navajo, Chinese, Vietnamese, ASL, Apache, etc.) as needed.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.q.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: Elect - DEM PC Appointments
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Candace Annette Kelley, Jean Brooke Stackey, Mardi Ann Harris, Arthur
Joe Kelley, JR., Wayne Lee Pearce, Luther H. Lee, Susan J. Etsitty, Martha Pearce and
Penny West, as Democratic Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2020. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility;
vacancy; duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an
election to fill precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of
precinct committeeman positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election
for those positions not sooner than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the
person who filed the nominating petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a
nominating petition to fill a position, the position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as
otherwise provided by law. A precinct committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this
subsection after filing a nominating petition shall be deemed an elected precinct
committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is two years and begins
on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman was a
candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.

MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT 
Between 
Navajo County Probation Department 
And 
The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board 
This Memorandum of Understanding (“MOA”), effective upon approval of all parties, is between 
Navajo County Probation Department (NCPD) and The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce 
Development Board (NEAZ LWDB). The MO A will expire June 30, 2020 unless cancelled prior 
to that date in writing.  This Agreement may be renewed for a period of one year by mutual 
agreement of the parties. This MOA may be cancelled at any time by either party giving 90 days 
written notice to the other party. The MO A may be modified at any time by written modificatio n 
mutually agreed upon by both parties. 
1. Purpose: Provide for a partnership between NCPD and the NEAZ LWDB to cross-train Youth 
Care Workers working within the Navajo County Instructional School (NCIS)  to provide 
ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona Youth Workforce Specialists services to at -risk 
youth. 
 
2. Organizational Responsibilities: 
Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board 
1. Contribute up to twenty-five percent (25%) of the salary and employee related benefits for 
two Youth Care Workers, up to a maximum contribution of $30,381.00 for the first contract 
year.  
2. Designate a primary contact person to address Youth Care Worker duties, work load, and 
work load balance. 
3. Review and evaluate program performance at least annually to ensure provision of 
expected quality of service. 
4. Provide pre-service and in-service training, technical assistance, and staff development 
opportunities for the Youth Care Workers. 
5. Monitor and evaluate program development and pr ogress toward meeting enrollment and 
performance goals. 
Navajo County Probation Department 
1. Manage the training process for the Youth Care Workers. 
2. Provide two Youth Care Workers to provide Workforce Specialist services to at-risk youth 
in concert with their regular duties. 
3. Provide the employment contract for the Youth Care Workers. 
4. Designate a primary contact person to address Youth Care Worker duties, work load, and 
work load balance.

<!-- page 552 -->
5. Provide employee related benefits for the Youth Care Workers  in the same manner 
provided to other regular employees. 
6. Provide office space, furnishings, equipment and supplies for the Youth Care Workers, as 
necessary. 
7. Provide pre-service and in-service trai ning, technical assistance, and staff development 
opportunities for the Youth Care Worker. 
8. Monitor and evaluate program development and progress toward meeting enrollment and 
performance goals. 
 
3. Funding & Billing: 
 
1. The NEAZ LWDB agrees to pay 25% of the salary and employee related benefits for two 
Youth Care Workers/Workforce Specialists, not to exceed $ 30,381.00 for the first contact 
year, with NCPD paying 75% of the salary and employee related benefits.  
 
4. Miscellaneous: 
 
1. Each party and its employees and agents shall maintain confidentiality of and safeguard all 
confidential information of the other party including without limitation, client informatio n, 
business practices, information systems, security passwords, financial inform ation and 
property information (collectively referred to as “Confidential Information”), and shall not 
disclose such Confidential Information or make it available to any person, or use it in any 
way other than as contemplated by this Agreement. Each party’s obligations to maintain 
and safeguard, not to disclose such Confidential Information or make it available to any 
person or use it in any way other than as contemplated by this Agreement.  Each party shall 
further report to the other party any use or disclosure of Confidential Information that it 
becomes aware of and which is not authorized by this Agreement.  Each party’s obligatio ns 
to maintain and safeguard, not to disclose, and to report unauthorized disclosures of 
Confidential Information shall survive the termination of this agreement.  
2. Neither NCPD or NEAZ LWDB shall use the name of t he other party or its employees in 
any publicity or advertising material without prior written approval by a duly authorized 
representative of the other party.  
3. The parties agree to comply with all applicable state and federal laws, rules, regulatio ns 
and executive orders governing equal employment opportunity, immigration, and 
nondiscrimination. 
4. Conflict of Interest. This Agreement is subject to Section 38 -511 of the Arizona Revised 
Statutes.  This Agreement may be canceled if any person significantly invo lved in 
initiating, negotiating, securing, drafting, or creating this Agreement on behalf of either 
party is and employee, consultant, or agent of any other party to this Agreement.  
5. If a dispute arises under this Agreement, the parties agree to exhaust al l applicable 
administrative remedies. 
6. Each party under this Agreement shall maintain adequate insurance to cover its obligatio ns 
under this Agreement.

<!-- page 553 -->
7. If NCPD or N EAZ L WDB’s performance under this Agreement depends upon the 
appropriation of funds by any o ther entity, and if any of these entities fail to appropriate 
the funds necessary for performance, then either party may provide written notice of 
cancellation to cancel this Agreement without further obligatio n.  
 
5. General Agreement: 
 
1. This Agreement constitutes the entire agreement and understanding of the parties with 
respect to its subject matter.  No prior or contemporaneous agreement or understanding 
will be effective.  This agreement shall be governed by the laws of Arizona, the co urts of 
which state shall have jurisdiction over its subject matter.

<!-- page 562 -->
About the District: 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District (HOFD) is a Fire District providing fire, Emergency Medical 
Services and transport service to citizens in an unincorporated area of Navajo County. The 
District provides ambulance services to a 200 square mile area surrounding the communities of 
Heber-Overgaard, including 55 miles of State Highway and 460 miles of rural roads. Navajo 
County collects the property tax revenues for the Fire District and maintains the bank account for 
the District. The Fire District partners with Navajo County on many projects.  
HOFD has a significant wildland fire threat where 268 structures were lost during the Rodeo-
Chediski fires and over 2,000 residents were evacuated for over two weeks. Last year the 377-
fire consumed 5,414 acres and left the Heber-Overgaard area without power for three days. 
HOFD is seeking funding to create a civilian and firefighter safety initiative and due to the 
district’s low levy of 1.81% this is a nonbudgetable item. 
 
Program goals: 
To add necessary equipment for public and firefighter safety through Rapid Extraction Module 
Support (REMS). The REMS is a pre-staged rescue team assigned to a wildland fire to provide 
firefighters a safe, effective and efficient method of egress off the fireline in the event of injury 
or illness incurred during firefighting operations. Wildland firefighting is an inherently 
dangerous profession. While safety is the primary concern during all operations, unintended 
incidents do occur which result in injury or illness to firefighters. It is the intent of the REMS to 
provide firefighters, who are unable to egress under their own power, a safe and secure transport 
off the fireline while simultaneously receiving the appropriate medical attention. Ideal conditions 
may not exist due to a number of circumstances such as heavy smoke inversion, no roads, or 
equipment malfunctions. REMS provide incident managers another option to reach incapacitated 
firefighters, with fully equipped resources, prepared to package and transport injured or ill 
personnel off the fireline to the appropriate medical care unit.  
Extraction, Medical Treatment, and Transport REMS travel with extraction and medical 
equipment and bring UTV. The ability to transport increases significantly because of the UTV, 
not just because it can get to more remote locations but also because it can carry more equipment 
closer to the patient.  “We stabilize and treat while transporting. This is our biggest advantage”. 
 
Results measured: 
The REMS program historically is a new program implemented in 2012 on the national scale to 
increase civilian and firefighter survival rates in wildland urban interface events. Most situations 
where a REMS was deployed were life threatening situations. As such, wilderness/wildland fire 
rescue procedures have adapted to become flexible in order to rescue an injured firefighter in a 
timely manner. This need was realized on incidents like the Rough Fire, Freezeout Ridge and 
others where traditional rescue procedures and/or equipment was unavailable or ineffective. The 
Heber-Overgaard Fire District measure results through professional national certifications in 
rope rescue, Paramedicine, education and the certified continuing education to stay proficient. 
Deployment response is recorded and documented through multiple systems of reporting. After 
action reviews are conducted post every incident.

<!-- page 563 -->
Indicators of success:  
Firefighter safety is a top priority when working to control wildland fires. We are always 
prepared for an emergency. The Rapid Extraction Module Support team specializes in getting 
injured firefighters out of the rough terrain safely to a location where traditional transport 
methods can be used. Where vehicle access isn’t an option, the team uses a combination of rope 
systems and a portable rescue stretcher with a wheel attached to the underside to safely extract 
the patient. 
When not being used for a rescue on an incident, members train with equipment and instruct 
other firefighters how to stabilize an injury until the team arrives. The team also scouts out areas 
where the firefighters are working so they know the best ways to access them and transport them 
safely off the fireline. Education is a large component of injury prevention which the reduction 
of reportable injuries or no injuries on an incident is an indicator of success.  
 
 
Current source of funding: 
The Heber-Overgaard tax rate is 1.81% for a small community the Department prides itself on 
remaining fiscally responsible. This however does not allow for a major equipment purchase. 
The department will provide personnel, training, rescue equipment and medical supplies to 
initiate the program. Sustainability of the program will reside with the Heber -Overgaard Fire 
District. UTV deployment and maintenance records will be available for reporting to the Pascua 
Yaqui Nation. This is a new project and no other Tribal funding has been sought for this 
program.

Project Budget Detail: 
1. Polaris Ranger Crew 1000 is the primary transport vehicle for the program. This 
apparatus was compared to two other brands which were very similar but the Polaris 
Government buying program was over a $700.00 savings.  
2. Winch will provide self-escape capabilities in wilderness responses. 
3. Rescue skid with attendant seat is how an injured patient is transported in the bed of the 
apparatus. The Paramedic is seated in the attendant seat able to provide advanced life 
saving skills while the patient is secured preventing any further injury.  
4. Hood storage rack to secure equipment and medications in transport. 
5. Certified and warrantied manufacture installation.   
6. Rope rescue and wildland equipment purchased by the Heber-Overgaard Fire District to 
compliment the program. 
7. Nationally accredited and to National Fire Protection Agency 1670 standards for 
operating personnel. 
8. Trailer to haul UTV obtained by the District used in local equipment movement.

Re:  Revenue Sharing Application by the Heber-Overgaard Fire District 
 
To Whom It May Concern: 
 
Navajo County is in support of the Revenue Sharing Application submitted by the 
Heber-Overgaard Fire District for the Rapid Extraction Module Support (REMS) 
program.  The REMS program will add necessary equipment for public and firefighter 
safety.   
 
REMS is a pre-staged rescue team assigned to a wildland fire to provide firefighters a 
safe, effective and efficient method of egress off the fireline in the event of injury or 
illness incurred during firefighting operations.  Wildland firefighting is a dangerous 
profession, and while safety is the primary concern during all operations, unintended 
incidents do occur which result in injury or illness to firefighters. 
 
The proposed project appears to avoid duplication problems, and the Heber-Overgaard 
Fire District will coordinate with appropriate partners to ensure efficient 
and effective use of grant funds. 
 
Based on past experience with the Heber-Overgaard Fire District, Navajo County has 
confidence and is in full support of the revenue sharing application and encourages its 
acceptance.

Background:
The Arizona Department of Forestry and Fire Management (the “Department”) and the Navajo
County Board of Supervisors (the “County”) enter into this Agreement to establish the terms and
conditions under which the Department shall disburse to the County monies that have been
appropriated by the Arizona Legislature to the Department for environmental county grants.
Attachments
DFFM & Navajo County ECO Agreement 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 05/10/2019 12:45 PM

<!-- page 571 -->
Page 1 of 5 
 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
 
between 
 
ARIZONA DEPARTMENT OF FORESTRY AND FIRE MANAGEMENT 
 
and 
 
THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
regarding 
 
ENVIRONMENTAL PROGRAM ASSISTANCE 
 
 
I. PURPOSE 
 
The Arizona Department of Forestry and Fire Management (the “Department”) and the 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors (the “County”) enter into this Agreement to 
establish the terms and conditions under which the Department shall disburse to the 
County monies that have been appropriated by the Arizona Legislature to the Department 
for environmental county grants. 
 
II. RECITALS 
 
A. The Fiscal Year 2019 Arizona State budget includes an appropriation in the sum 
of $250,000 to the Department for environmental county grants.  
 
B. In previous fiscal years, monies for environmental county grants have been 
equally distributed to Greenlee, Graham, Gila, Navajo, and Apache Counties 
(“eastern Arizona Counties”) for environmental projects that impact economic 
development in these counties. 
 
C. To streamline the administration of these grants, the Department intends to 
distribute to Navajo county (serving as fiscal agent for the Eastern Arizona 
Counties Organization) environmental county grant monies appropriated and 
made available to the Department, subject to the County submitting a proposed 
plan to the Department that is approved by the Arizona State Forester. 
 
D. By this Agreement, the Department and the County now wish to confirm the terms 
and conditions under which the Department shall disburse environmental county 
grant monies to the County. 
 
E. This Agreement shall become effective upon execution by both parties and shall 
be for a term of five (5) years commencing on its effective date, unless terminated 
at an earlier date as provided for in this Agreement.

<!-- page 572 -->
Page 2 of 5 
 
 
III. STIPULATIONS 
 
A. TERMS OF DISBURSEMENT 
 
1. Amount - The Department agrees to disburse to the County the sum of 
$250,000 during fiscal year 2019 for the purposes of planning and 
implementing specific environmental programs through coordination with 
Eastern Arizona Counties Organization.  The Department and the County 
agree that the monies will be disbursed as stipulated in Paragraph 3 of this 
Subsection. In the subsequent fiscal years during the term of this 
Agreement, the Department agrees to disburse to the County the full sum 
of the appropriation made to the Department for environmental county 
grants, provided the Arizona Legislature appropriates and makes available 
such monies to the Department (See Section IV, Subsection H of this 
Agreement) and County satisfies all of its obligations under this 
Agreement.  
 
2. Proposed Plans - The County shall prepare and submit proposed plans for 
approval by the Department on or before December 31st of the funded 
fiscal year.  The County shall submit documentation of completed 
projects. Proposed plans and documentation of completed projects shall 
include: 
 
a. Description of the projects undertaken, including their relevance 
and contribution to the Program. 
 
b. For each project, a budget itemized by specific categories of 
expenditures. 
 
c. Specific goals, objectives and finished products delivered or 
achieved by the proposed projects. 
 
d. A completion date for each project. 
 
3. Disbursement - The Department shall disburse the appropriated monies to 
the County as follows: 
 
a. After approving the County’s plan pursuant to Subsection B, 
Paragraph 2 of this Agreement, the Department shall release to the 
County the full sum of the monies appropriated to the Department 
for environmental county grants for the funded fiscal year. 
. 
B. DEPARTMENT OBLIGATIONS 
 
1. Plan Review and Approval - Before disbursing any portion of the

<!-- page 573 -->
Page 3 of 5 
 
appropriated monies, the Department shall review the proposed plans 
submitted by the County, including any plan amendments, to ensure their 
consistency with the appropriation legislation, the Program Plan, and this 
Agreement. 
 
a. If the Department finds that the proposed plans are substantially 
consistent with the appropriation legislation, the Program Plan, and 
this Agreement, the Department shall approve the plans in writing 
and disburse the monies as provided under Section III, Subsection 
A, Paragraph 3 of this Agreement. 
 
b. If the Department finds that the proposed plans are not 
substantially consistent with the appropriation legislation, the 
Program Plan, or this Agreement, the Department shall request that 
the County amend the plan before the Department provides written 
approval and disburses the monies. 
 
2. Review and Inspection Terms 
 
a. Reviews of proposed plans and project documentation pursuant to 
this subsection shall be in the sole and reasonable discretion of the 
Department subject to the intent of the appropriation legislation. 
 
b. On reasonable notice to the County, the Department may inspect 
all aspects of any project to ensure consistency with plans approved 
pursuant to Paragraph 1 of this subsection, the Program Plan, this 
Agreement, and the appropriation legislation. 
 
C. COUNTY OBLIGATIONS 
 
In addition to preparing and submitting proposed plans for approval by the 
Department as required, the County shall: 
 
1. Prepare and submit documentation of projects and return to the 
Department any monies which remain unspent after the end of the fiscal 
year in which the funds are disbursed. 
 
2. Be solely responsible for completing projects funded pursuant to this 
Agreement and for satisfying any third-party contracts or agreements. 
 
3. Be solely responsible to the Department for the accounting of monies 
disbursed pursuant to this Agreement.

<!-- page 574 -->
Page 4 of 5 
 
4. Use monies disbursed by the Department pursuant to this Agreement only 
for projects which have been approved by the Department as provided 
under Section III, Subsection B, Paragraph 1, of this Agreement. 
 
5. On the Department’s demand, remit any portion of previously disbursed 
monies that is not used according to the terms and conditions of this 
Agreement. 
 
6. Comply with all applicable federal and state laws, rules and regulations. 
 
7. Appoint a representative for compliance with this Agreement who, on the 
Department’s request, shall certify that: 
 
a. The representative has the authority to act in and on behalf of the 
County. 
 
b. The county shall provide all financial and managerial resources 
necessitated by projects funded pursuant to this Agreement. 
 
c. The County is in compliance with the terms and conditions of this 
Agreement. 
 
IV. ADDITIONAL TERMS 
 
A. INDEMNIFICATION - The County agrees to assume all risks of loss and to 
indemnify and hold the State of Arizona, the Department, its officers, agents 
employees and attorneys harmless from and against any liabilities, demands, 
claims, suits, losses, damages, causes of action, fines or judgements, including 
costs, attorneys’ and witness’ fees and expenses thereto, for injury or death to 
persons and for loss of, damage to, theft of or destruction of any personal or real 
property, including loss of use thereof arising from or in connection with the 
performance of the County’s obligations pursuant to this Agreement, whether or 
not authorized by the Department. 
 
B. DEFAULT - In the event of a default by either party of the terms of this 
Agreement, the non-defaulting party shall have all rights and remedies available at 
law. 
 
C. TERMINATION - By written notice, the Department may terminate this 
Agreement in whole or in part, without penalty. 
 
D. RECORDS - All books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this 
Agreement shall be subject, at all reasonable times, to inspection and audit by the 
State for five years after the termination of this Agreement.  Upon request, County 
shall produce such records at the offices of the Department.

<!-- page 575 -->
Page 5 of 5 
 
E. CONFLICT OF INTEREST - This Agreement is subject to cancellation by the 
Governor or the County pursuant to A.R.S. Section 38-511. 
 
F. ARBITRATION - To the extent required by A.R.S. Section 12 -1518, the parties 
agree to use arbitration, after exhausting applicable administrative reviews, to 
resolve disputes arising from this Agreement. 
 
G. ANTI-DISCRIMINATION - The parties agree to comply with Chapter 9, Title 41, 
Arizona Revised Statutes (Civil Rights), Arizona Executive Order No. 2009-09, 
and any other federal or State laws relating to equal opportunity and non-
discrimination, including the Americans with Disabilities Act. 
 
H. UNAVAILABILITY OF FUNDS - Every payment obligation of the Department 
under this Agreement is conditioned upon the availability of funds appropriated 
and allocated for the payment of such obligation.  If funds are not appropriated, 
allocated and available, or if the appropriation is changed by the legislature 
resulting in funds no longer being available for the continuance of this Agreement, 
this Agreement may be terminated by the Department at the end of the period for 
which funds are available.  No liability shall accrue to the Department or any 
other agency of the State of Arizona in the event this provision is exercised, and 
neither the Department nor any other agency of the State of Arizona shall be 
obligated or liable for any future payments or for any damages as a result of 
termination under this paragraph. 
 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the foregoing Agreement is executed on the date of the last signature 
shown below.

4.5. Activity Five (5) Immunization Quality Improvement 
 
4.5.1. AIPO will provide quality improvement assistance to the Contractor, on an annual basis, as part of the 
Immunization Quality Improvement for Providers (IQIP) program. This program replaces the current 
Assessment, Feedback, Incentives and eXchange (AFIX) program, per CDC directives, on July 1, 
2019. As part of the IQIP requirements, the Contractor shall receive the following assistance: 
 
4.5.1.1. An annual in-person site visit from AIPO staff to include an ASIIS-based coverage rate 
report for children ages 24-35 months and for adolescents aged thirteen (13) years, 
including a list of patients not up-to-date. The visit will include a discussion of current 
immunization practices and quality improvement goals; 
 
4.5.1.2. Phone-based check-in calls at two (2) months and six (6) months post-site visit to include 
a discussion of the quality improvement objectives and any technical assistance 
requested by the Contractor; and 
 
4.5.1.3. An email-based check-in at twelve (12) months post-site visit, to include a follow-up 
coverage rate assessment, a discussion of progress toward quality improvement goals, 
and any other technical assistance requested by the Contractor. 
 
4.5.2. Removed.

RESOLUTION ____-19 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS TO 
PARTICIPATE IN THE CENSUS 2020 COMPLETE COUNT COMMITTEE AND TO 
ENCOURAGE EVERY PERSON TO BE COUNTED 
 
 
WHEREAS The United States Constitution requires a Census of the population of our nation 
every 10 years. The Federal Government uses the Census information to determine how many 
Representatives the State of Arizona has in the United States House of Representatives, and the 
State Government uses the information to determine districting in our State Legislature; and  
 
WHEREAS, individual Census records are confidential and protected under Title 13 of the United 
States Code. Under the census laws, the Department of Commerce and its Bureau of the Census are 
prohibited from sharing individual Census information with any other agency, including for 
example, immigration or law enforcement agencies; and 
 
WHEREAS, having an accurate and complete Census count is important to Navajo County in 
determining Federal and State aids and grants, economic development, housing assistance, 
transportation improvements and many other uses; and 
 
WHEREAS, all units of government, large and small corporations, non-profit organizations, 
religious organization and local businesses of all sizes rely on available information on population 
and family size, and income data to further government investment, job development, and 
marketing guidance and to determine the impact of education, health, and social programs; and 
 
WHEREAS, Navajo County, the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside, City of Show Low, Town of Taylor, 
Town of Snowflake, City of Holbrook and City of Winslow have each pledged that every resident 
of Navajo County counts and deserves to be counted, and their participation in the Census 2020 
Complete Count Committee reflects their ideals in furtherance of that pledge; and 
  
WHEREAS, a united voice for businesses, government, community-based and faith-based 
organizations, educators, media and others will allow the 2020 Census message to reach a broader 
audience, and Complete Count Committees are a core strategic element of the Census Bureau to 
promote the Census; and 
 
WHEREAS, Complete Count Committees exist to plan and implement locally-based outreach 
campaigns that raise awareness of the Census and are comprised of trusted voices in the community.

<!-- page 585 -->
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF 
SUPERVISORS AS FOLLOWS:  
 
• The Board r ecognizes the importance of the 2020 Census and establishes the County’s 
participation in the regional Navajo County Complete Count Committee. 
 
• The Board asks its affiliates and membership to partner together to achieve an accurate and 
complete count. 
 
• The Board e ncourages membership and affiliates to participa te in events and initiatives 
including local Complete Count Committees that will raise overall awareness of the 2020 
Census and increase participation among all populations. 
 
• The Board w ill promote Census 2020 Jobs through all means the County determines to be 
effective.  
 
• The Board w ill use 2020 Census drop -in articles, messages and logos in appropriate 
newsletters, mailings and other in-house communications.  
 
• The Board will provide a link to the 2020 Census website from the Navajo County website.  
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Superviso rs on this 
___ day of ___________, 2019.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairman of the Board 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: Proclamation Declaring May 19-25 National Public Works Week in Navajo County
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring the week of May 19-25, 2019 as National Public
Works Week in Navajo County.
Background:
The American Public Works Association has sponsored National Public Works Week
since 1960.  Communities throughout North America use this week to remind the public
of the importance of public works in their daily lives.  For Navajo County, vital Public
Works, Facility Maintenance, and Emergency Management operations and services
include: 
Roadway maintenance and snow removal operations
Tribal transportation project and community cleanup coordination
Administrative business and governmental finance services
Facility maintenance and custodial operations
Emergency Management Coordination
Technical engineering and flood control services
Building and zoning inspections
Planning and zoning services
Fleet maintenance operations 
In addition to educating and reminding the public about the importance of public works
services, recognition of National Public Works Week in Navajo County offers the
opportunity to thank and acknowledge the outstanding work of the men and women of our
Public Works, Facility Maintenance, and Emergency Management work teams.  Not only
do they provide daily professional public works services which protect the health and
safety and enhance the quality of life of Navajo County residents, businesses and visitors,
many of our employees are emergency responders during snow storms, fires, floods,
traffic accidents and other public safety incidents.  Given the outstanding contributions
our employees make in protecting our health, safety, quality of life and essential built
infrastructure, all the people of Navajo County are encouraged to join with representatives
of the American Public Works Association in paying tribute to the men and women of the
Navajo County Public Works and Facility Maintenance Departments.

<!-- page 592 -->
Recommendation: 
Approve the proclamation and declare May 19-25, 2019 National Public Works Week in Navajo
County
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N: N/A
Amount Requested: N/A
Fiscal Impact:
There is no fiscal impact for this item
Attachments

<!-- page 593 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
National Public Works Week 
WHEREAS, public works professionals focus on infrastructure, facilities and services that are of vital importance to 
sustainable and resilient communities and to the public health, high quality of life and well-being of the people of Navajo 
County; and 
WHEREAS, these infrastructure projects, maintenance operations, emergency management coordination and essential 
services could not be provided without the dedicated efforts of public works professionals from s tate and local 
governments and the private sector; and 
Whereas these public works professionals are responsible for rebuilding, improving and protecting our nation’s 
transportation system; water supply; water treatment and solid waste systems; public buildings; and other structures and 
facilities essential for our citizens; and 
WHEREAS, the men and women of  the Navajo County Public Works, Facility Maintenance and Emergency Management 
teams excel in protecting our infrastructure and the health and safety of  residents, businesses, and visitors by expertly 
providing roadway maintenance and snow removal operations; administrative business and governmental finance 
services; facility maintenance and custodial operations; emergency management coordination; technical engineering and 
flood control services; building and zoning inspections; tribal transportation project and community cleanup coordination; 
planning and zoning services; and fleet maintenance operations;  and 
WHEREAS, many of our employees are emergency responders 24/7 during snow storms, fires, floods, traffic accidents and 
other public safety incidents; and 
WHEREAS, these dedicated employees strive to embody the Navajo County values of Teamwork, Accountability, Integrity, 
Excellence and Innovation every day while providing superior public service.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, that May 19-25, 2019 is 
designated as “National Public Works Week” in our community and urge all the people of Navajo County to join 
with representatives of the American Public Works Association to pay tribute to the men and women of Navajo 
County Public Works, Facility Maintenance, and Emergency Management for the outstanding contributions they 
make in protecting our health, safety, and quality of life.  
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on May 14, 2019. 
         ____________________________ 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman 
 
ATTEST: 
_________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.b.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: BIA 157 Gravel Placement
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 16 to Memorandum of Agreement between the
Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation- Navajo Region and Navajo County for
the funding of purchasing aggregate material and construction water to be delivered to BIA route
157 between the Indian Wells and Greasewood Community. 
Background:
The Bureau of Indian Affairs Department of Transportation has requested some assistance on
providing roadway material for segments along their inventoried route 157 that travels between
Greasewood and Indian Wells, Arizona. Throughout this past winter season, BIA Fort Defiance
Agency has received multiple requests for repair assistance on this road. Elders and high risk
patients have difficulty travelling during inclement weather because sections of the road become
impassable. So the agency engineer has made improvement of the roadway conditions a priority
by applying a gravel material in certain segments which will allow community members to travel
across muddy roads.  As stated in the letter of request, BIA forces will provide the labor, tools and
equipment to place the material.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends that the Board of Supervisors approve the attached Task Agreement between
BIA DOT and Navajo County.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $40,000
Fiscal Impact:
Allocated $40,000 from District 2 Special Road Funds.

Budgeted Y/N: Y

Task Agreement NO. 16 
Bureau of Indian Affairs- Fort Defiance Agency 
157 Gravel Placement 
 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular 
meeting on May 14, 2019, has approved the expenditure of up to $90,000 in (X) Special 
Project Road Funds, or () Highway User Revenue Funds to be used for funding the 
purchase of Class 6 Aggregate Material & Construction Water for road maintenance 
activity on public BIA Route N157 , which is located on the Navajo Indian Reservation 
within BIA Fort Defiance Agency, in Navajo County’s Supervisor District No. 1 and 2. 
The Funds shall be used to purchase and deliver the gravel aggregate/ water to 
segments on BIA Route N157, Indian Wells and Greasewood, Arizona.  
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted from 
projects within this area or allocated to projects outside this area. The expenditure of the 
Allocated Funds shall only include the acquisition of delivering gravel material and 
providing construction water. 
The BIA warrants and represents that the material purchased with the Allocated Funds 
will be used primarily for maintenance activities on roads within District 1 and 2, which 
are open to the public and are eligible for the expenditure of HURF monies under the 
Arizona Constitution and related Arizona Revised Statutes. The BIA to fully cooperate in 
any audit of the Allocated Funds by the Arizona Auditor General.  
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of June 30, 2019 shall no longer be available 
for distribution pursuant to this Memorandum of Agreement. The Allocated Funds under 
this agreement shall be paid by Navajo County directly to the invoicing party upon the 
County’s receipt of invoices or billing statement that show that the items have been 
purchased and approved by the BIA. The total of all invoices shall not exceed the 
approved amount from this agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.c.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: Roadway Easement Procurement for School House Lane in Linden
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of authorizing Easement and Land Purchase Agreement with the James M. Lando
Trust for the School House Lane Roadway Easement.
Background:
Residents along School House Lane and Linden Elementary School have requested
Navajo County to maintain School House Lane.  In order to complete this request, a 35'
Public Roadway Easements needs to be purchased from the James M. Lando Trust to have
a 50' roadway easement width adjacent to the Linden Elementary School.  Navajo County
Public Works has worked closely with Mr. James Lando to provide him with easement
details and the Easement and Land Purchase Agreement.
An appraisal for the easement was completed by KB Valuation Service from Mesa,
Arizona.  They appraised the 35' roadway easement to be valued at $10,000.00.  The final
negotiated price with Mr. Lando was $14,000.00.
Purchasing the 35' Roadway from the Lando Trust will allow for the entire School House
Lane to be considered for County maintenance.  The roadway north ot the school will be
maintained as a graded road.  The existing asphalt pavement adjacent to the school will be
maintain which will help improve roadway safety issues for the parent drop off and bus
area. 
Public Works will submit an Agenda Request for the May 28, 2019 BOS meeting  for
consideration of acceptance of School House Lane as a County Maintained Road
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the approval to purchase the 35' easement from the Lando Trust
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N: y

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.d.        
Meeting Date: 05/14/2019  
Title: IGA with the Town of Snowflake to Partner on Preventive Maintenance Road
Projects
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of an Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Snowflake authorizing
Navajo County to provide financial assistance for the purchase of materials, labor and
equipment to be used to chip seal various streets in the Town of Snowflake.  
Background:
The Town of Snowflake has requested assistance with their annual preventive
maintenance plan for approximately 4.8 miles of chip sealing on the following roadways: 
 
  Park Plaza
  Ridgeview
  Fairway
  Snowflake Heights
  Hillside
  Mountain View
  Sundown
Navajo County and Town of Snowflake staff have met to discuss the project, developed a
scope of work, and agreed to share equally in the total cost of the project; Navajo County
road maintenance forces will perform the work.  Partnering on these types of projects is
anticipated in the County's CIP through district special road project allocations 
Recommendation: 
Approve the IGA.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY19&FY20
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $80,000
Fiscal Impact:

<!-- page 613 -->
Not to exceed $80,000.  The maintenance work will be performed in spring, 2019 (approximately $20K) and
fall, 2019 (approximately $60K) over both fiscal years.

IGA Snowflake Chip Seal 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Brandt Clark Brandt Clark 05/09/2019 08:57 PM

<!-- page 614 -->
This Intergovernmental Agreement (“IGA”) is made by and between the Town of Snowflake , an 
Arizona municipal corporation (“the Town”), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the 
State of Arizona (“County”), pursuant to the parties’ authority under A.R.S §11 -952.  
1. PURPOSE  
The Purpose of this IGA is to authorize the County to provide financial assistance to the 
Town for the purchase of materials, labor and equipment to be used to chip seal various 
streets in the Town of Snowflake .  Financial participation  and maintenance partnership s 
of this kind are consistent with the County’s Board approved policy of utilizing HUR F 
funds to supplement HURF eligible roadway maintenance activities throughout the 
County, including tribal lands. This IGA supersedes any conflicting Agreements recorded  
between the same parties.  
  
2. EFFECTIVE DATE AND TERM   
This Agreement shall be effective on the date on which it is recorded by the Office of the 
Navajo County Recorder.  This Agreement shall remain in full force and in effect for a  
one (1) year period commencing on its effective date.    
  
3. TERMS, CONDITIONS, AND FINANCING   
a. The County agrees to purchase materials and provide labor to do the chip seal work  
on approximately  4.8 miles of roadway  in an amount not to exceed $80,000.  Work 
will be performed on the following roadways:  
• Park Plaza 
• Ridgeview  
• Fairway 
• Snowflake Heights  
• Hillside  
• Mountain View  
• Sundown 
 
b. The Parties recognize that the performance of both Parties may be dependent upon 
the appropriation of funds by each Party’s governing, legislative authority. Should the 
County Board of Supervisors or the Town Council  in the case of the Town  fail to 
appropriate the necessary funds or if either Party’s applicable appropriation is 
reduced during the fiscal year, the Party that is subject to the reduced or eliminated 
funding may reduce the scope of this Agreement if appropriate or cancel this 
Agreement without further duty or obligation. Each Party agrees to notify the other 
Party as soon as reasonably possible after the unavailability of said funds comes to 
its attention.  
  
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT  
  
between  
NAVAJO COUNTY  
and  
THE TOWN OF SNOWFLAKE

<!-- page 615 -->
c.  Indemnification   
The Town agrees to indemnify, defend, and hold harmless the County, and its officers, 
agents and employees against all liability, loss, and costs arising from actions, suits, 
claims, or demands arising from performance of this contract.  
  
 d.  HURF Obligations   
The Town understands and acknowledges that the funds used to purchase the 
materials and labor for the project described above are  Arizona Highway User 
Revenue Fund (HURF) monies which are subject to audit by the Arizona Auditor 
General and assure s Navajo County tha t the work will be performed on HURF 
qualified roads.  The Town agrees to fully cooperate in any audit of the use of these 
funds by the Arizona Auditor General.  
 
4. TERMINATION  
a. Cancelation at Will  
Either party may terminate this IGA upon written notice to the other party at least 
ninety (90) days before the effective date of termination. Notice to the County shall be 
addressed to the Navajo County Manager and notice to the City shall be addressed to  
the Town of Snowflake Town Manager.      
  
b. Agreed Cancellation This IGA may be canceled by  mutual agreement.    
  
c. Violation of Agreement  
Either party may terminate this IGA if a violation of any of the terms and conditions 
by the other party remains uncured thirty (30) days after the terminating party has 
delivered to the violating party a written notice specifying the nature of the violati on.  
  
d. This Agreement is subject to cancellation pursuant to the provisions of A.R.S. 
§38511regarding Conflict of Interest.  
  
IN WITNESS WHEREOF , the authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
agreement pursuant to resolutions duly ad opted by their respective governing boards.   
  
            NAVAJO COUNTY  
  
            BY              
            Dawnafe Whitesinger , Chairwoman  
           Navajo County Board of Supervisors

Motion before the Board:

#B17-05-016, for road maintenance and related improvements, extending the contract for one
additional year to July 10, 2020 with no cost increase
Background:
On August 21, 2006, the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District was formed by Resolution
#68-06.  On July 11, 2017 the Board of Directors approved a contract with Perkins Cinders, Inc.
for road maintenance and related improvements.  This Contract may be renewed for up to four
additional one-year terms.  This contract has worked very well for this District.  Therefore, we are
recommending that the contract be extended one additional year (July 11, 2019 to July 10, 2020)
with no cost increase.
 
Concurrence has been received by the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Advisory
Committee and the Contractor.
